<a id="pm-s1"></a>
## 1. Abstract and result contract

> We prove the four-dimensional Singer conjecture: the <i>L</i><sup>2</sup>-Betti numbers of the universal cover of a closed connected aspherical topological four-manifold vanish outside degree two. More generally, the same vanishing holds for finite connected aspherical integral Poincaré complexes of formal dimension four, including nonorientable ones.

- **C1** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-main)): Let $Q$ be a finite connected aspherical integral Poincaré complex of formal dimension four, with arbitrary orientation character. Then $$b_p^{(2)}(\widetilde Q)=0\qquad(p\ge0,\ p\ne2).$$
  - Explanation: Establishes concentration of the universal-cover invariants in the middle degree under absolute integral duality.
- **C2** (theorem, [source](#stmt-cor-singer-manifolds)): Let $M$ be a closed connected aspherical topological four-manifold. Then $$b_p^{(2)}(\widetilde M)=0\qquad(p\ge0,\ p\ne2).$$
  - Explanation: Transfers the concentration theorem to closed aspherical topological manifolds by a finite homotopy model.
- **C3** (theorem, [source](#stmt-found-model)): The complex $Q$ is homotopy equivalent to a finite four-dimensional CW complex. The group $\Gamma$ is infinite, finitely presented, and torsion-free.* *Every closed connected aspherical topological four-manifold is homotopy equivalent to a finite connected aspherical integral Poincaré complex of formal dimension four.
  - Explanation: Supplies the finite homotopy model and the group properties required by the subsequent reductions.
- **C4** (theorem, [source](#stmt-found-comparison)): Let $\Lambda$ be a group and let $P_*$ be a free $\mathbb Z\Lambda$-resolution of $\mathbb Z$ whose terms in degrees $0,1,2$ are finitely generated. Suppose that a contractible space $Z$ has a free $\Lambda$-action for which its singular chains are free $\mathbb Z\Lambda$-modules. Then $$b_1^{(2)}(Z;\mathcal N(\Lambda)) =\dim_{\mathcal N(\Lambda)} \bigl(\ker\partial_1\cap\ker\partial_2^*\bigr),$$ where the operators on the right act on the finite Hilbert modules $\ell^2(\Lambda)\otimes_{\mathbb Z\Lambda}P_i$. The same space represents first reduced Hilbert cohomology for $d_0=\partial_1^*$ and $d_1=\partial_2^*$. The analogous comparison holds in every degree of a finite free cellular complex.
  - Explanation: Identifies algebraic homology dimension with a harmonic Hilbert kernel through chain comparison.
- **C5** (theorem, [source](#stmt-found-reduction)): For the Poincaré complex $Q$ fixed above, $$b_0^{(2)}(\widetilde Q)=b_4^{(2)}(\widetilde Q)=0, \qquad b_3^{(2)}(\widetilde Q)=b_1^{(2)}(\widetilde Q),$$ and $b_p^{(2)}(\widetilde Q)=0$ for $p>4$. Consequently it suffices to prove $b_1^{(2)}(\widetilde Q)=0$.
  - Explanation: Reduces concentration to vanishing in the first degree, including arbitrary orientation character.
- **C6** (theorem, [source](#stmt-found-duality)): The group $\Gamma$ satisfies $$H^i(\Gamma;k\Gamma)=0\quad(i=1,2).$$ For every subgroup $J\leq\Gamma$ there is an isomorphism $$H^1(\Gamma;k[\Gamma/J])\cong H_3(J;k).$$ In particular, this group vanishes when $J$ is finite or two-ended.
  - Explanation: Transfers local-coefficient duality to group-ring cohomology and subgroup homology.
- **C7** (theorem, [source](#stmt-found-ends)): The group $\Gamma$ is one-ended.
  - Explanation: Excludes multiple ends by correcting a finite-support gradient.
- **C8** (theorem, [source](#stmt-found-trees)): For an action of $\Gamma$ without inversions on a simplicial tree:* 1. *if the action has no fixed vertex, its edge stabilizers cannot all be finite or two-ended;* 2. *if the action is minimal and the tree has an edge, every edge stabilizer is neither finite nor two-ended.* *Consequently $\Gamma$ has no nontrivial splitting over a finite or two-ended subgroup.
  - Explanation: Obstructs tree actions whose stabilizers have the excluded end types.
- **C9** (theorem, [source](#stmt-found-gradient)): The space $\mathcal D$ is a closed real Hilbert subspace of $\ell^2(E(G);\mathbb R)$, and $\mathcal D_0\subseteq\mathcal D$. If $b_1^{(2)}(\widetilde Q)>0$, then $\mathcal D_0\ne\mathcal D$.
  - Explanation: Converts positive first von Neumann dimension into a proper inclusion of gradient spaces.
- **C10** (construction, [source](#stmt-compact-potential)): There exists a finite-energy function $f:\Gamma\to[0,1]$ which does not tend to a single constant outside finite subsets of $\Gamma$, and for which both $\{f<t\}$ and $\{f>t\}$ are connected vertex subgraphs whenever $0<t<1$ is regular for $f$. Every such $S(f,t)$ is dually connected.
  - Explanation: Produces the bounded energy minimizer with connected regular level sides.
- **C11** (theorem, [source](#stmt-compact-wall-components)): Every regular cut of a coordinate in $\mathcal F_0$ has finitely many dual components. A nonempty regular cut of a translate of $f$ is dually connected, and a regular cut of a singleton indicator is finite.
  - Explanation: Controls the number of dual components of base-coordinate cuts.
- **C12** (construction, [source](#stmt-compact-good-boxes)): Good boxes form a basis of the base-coordinate cube. Every good box has finitely many graph components on its vertex set. There is a countable $\Gamma$-invariant basis of good boxes.
  - Explanation: Provides coordinate boxes with finitely many vertex components.
- **C13** (theorem, [source](#stmt-compact-localized-energy)): Every member of $\mathcal F$ has finite energy. For almost every $t\in(0,1)$, $$\label{compact:shifted-walls} S(h_{O,U},t)\subseteq \bigcup_{i=1}^m \bigl(S(F_i,a_i+t)\cup S(F_i,b_i-t)\bigr),$$ where every displayed level is regular for its coordinate.
  - Explanation: Preserves finite energy when coordinates record individual box components.
- **C14** (theorem, [source](#stmt-compact-boundary)): The boundary $B$ is a nondegenerate compact connected metric space.
  - Explanation: Establishes the nondegenerate continuum boundary from one-endedness and finite energy.
- **C15** (theorem, [source](#stmt-compact-near-path)): For every $x\in B$ and every open neighborhood $W$ of $x$ in $X$, there is an open neighborhood $V\subset W$ of $x$ such that any two vertices in $V$ can be joined by a finite graph path whose vertices all lie in $W$.* *Equivalently, if $u_n,v_n\in\Gamma$ tend to the same $x\in B$, there are joining graph paths $P_n$ with $\sup\{\rho(w,x):w\text{ is a vertex of }P_n\}\to0$.
  - Explanation: Connects vertices near the same boundary point by graph paths inside a prescribed neighborhood.
- **C16** (theorem, [source](#stmt-dyn-length)): For every base coordinate $F$ and $g\in\Gamma$, $$\int_0^1\sum_{e\in S(F,t)}\sum_{e'\text{ near }e} \lambda(ge')\,dt =\sum_e \|dF(e)\|\sum_{e'\text{ near }e}\lambda(ge'). \label{eq:dyn-length}$$ This quantity is finite and tends to zero as $g\to\infty$. There is a constant $C$, independent of $g,F,t$, such that for every dually connected set $D$ of edges, $$\operatorname{diam}_{\rho}\{gv:v\text{ is an endpoint of an edge of }D\} \le C\sum_{e\in D}\sum_{e'\text{ near }e}\lambda(ge'). \label{eq:dyn-dual-diameter}$$ The sum of these diameter bounds over distinct dual components is bounded by the same expression with their union in place of $D$.
  - Explanation: Bounds translated cut diameters by near-edge lengths and proves decay of the integrated length pairing.
- **C17** (theorem, [source](#stmt-dyn-walls)): Suppose $g_n\to a\in B$. Given finitely many coordinates from the enlarged coordinate family, there is a subsequence such that, for almost every level of each coordinate, all endpoints of the $g_n$-translated straddling set tend uniformly to $a$. If also $g_n^{-1}\to b\in B$, the subsequence can be chosen so that the corresponding inverse-translated straddling sets tend uniformly to $b$.
  - Explanation: Collapses translated cuts along a suitable subsequence for almost every coordinate level.
- **C18** (theorem, [source](#stmt-dyn-convergence)): If $g_n\to a\in B$ and $g_n^{-1}\to b\in B$, then $$g_nx\longrightarrow a \quad\text{uniformly for $x$ in each compact subset of }X\setminus\{b\}. \label{eq:dyn-convergence}$$ Consequently the action of $\Gamma$ on the space of distinct triples of $B$ is properly discontinuous; in particular, its action on $B$ is a convergence action.
  - Explanation: Obtains compact-uniform convergence away from the repelling point and proper discontinuity on triples.
- **C19** (theorem, [source](#stmt-dyn-parabolic)): Let $h\in\Gamma$ be parabolic with fixed point $p\in B$. Then $h^n\to p$ and $h^{-n}\to p$ as vertices of $X$. Both sequences of maps converge to $p$ uniformly on compact subsets of $X\setminus\{p\}$ and pointwise on all of $B$. Furthermore, $$\rho(g,gp)\longrightarrow0\qquad(g\longrightarrow\infty\text{ in }\Gamma). \label{eq:dyn-parabolic-comparison}$$
  - Explanation: Compares parabolic fixed-point images with escaping group vertices.
- **C20** (theorem, [source](#stmt-dyn-minimal)): The action of $\Gamma$ on $B$ is minimal: every orbit is dense.
  - Explanation: Excludes a global fixed point and establishes orbit density.
- **C21** (bound, [source](#stmt-topo-thin-walls)): For every coordinate $F$, the compact set $E(F,t)$ has $\mathcal H^1_\rho$-measure zero for almost every $t\in(0,1)$.
  - Explanation: Controls boundary accumulation of regular cuts by vanishing Hausdorff measure.
- **C22** (bound, [source](#stmt-topo-dimension)): The boundary $B$ has covering dimension at most one.
  - Explanation: Constructs arbitrarily fine covers with bounded multiplicity to control boundary dimension.
- **C23** (theorem, [source](#stmt-topo-cech)): With coefficients $k=\mathbb F_2$, one has $\check H^1(B;k)=0$.
  - Explanation: Kills boundary cover cocycles through a finite-support cellular correction.
- **C24** (construction, [source](#stmt-pret-item-fixed)): Let a group act minimally by convergence on a nondegenerate compact metric continuum $B$, and let $P\ne\varnothing$ be its set of global cut points. There is a complete median pretree $\Phi$ containing $P$ equivariantly, with the same betweenness on $P$, with these properties.* 1. * Two distinct points are called adjacent if their open interval is empty. Every adjacent pair in $\Phi$ has one point in $P$ and one outside $P$. A full subset whose intervals are finite is the vertex set of a simplicial tree, with these adjacent pairs as edges.* 2. * There is an equivariant map $\phi:B\to\Phi$, equal to the identity on $P$, and, for every $q\in\Phi$, an equivariantly assigned nonempty compact subset $R_B(q)\subset B$. For $q\in P$ this set is $\{q\}$; for $q\notin P$ it satisfies $$\label{pret:support} R_B(q)=\phi^{-1}(q)\cup\Lambda(q), \qquad \Lambda(q)=\{p\in P:p\text{ is adjacent to }q\}.$$ No point of $\Phi$ is fixed by the whole group.* 3. * Starting with equality on $\Phi$, successively collapse finite-interval classes (two points belong to one such class when their closed interval in the current quotient is finite), and at limit ordinals take the union of the preceding equivalence relations. This process stabilizes. Its final quotient is the canonical dendritic quotient $D(B)$ of $B$. In particular, $D(B)$ is a dendrite and there is a continuous equivariant surjection $B\to D(B)$. The induced action on $D(B)$ is minimal and convergence.* 4. *If a loxodromic element fixes $q,r\in\Phi$ with $[q,r]$ infinite, then $q,r$ are terminal in $\Phi$: neither lies strictly between two points. If a parabolic element has its unique fixed point in $B\setminus P$, then it has exactly one fixed point in $\Phi$.
  - Explanation: Packages the cut-point pretree, compact supports and successive interval collapses.
- **C25** (theorem, [source](#stmt-pret-parabolic-cuts)): Under the assumptions of this section, $D(B)$ is a point and every $p\in P$ has infinite stabilizer containing no loxodromic element. In particular, each such stabilizer contains a parabolic element of infinite order fixing $p$.
  - Explanation: Forces the dendritic quotient to be trivial and cut-point stabilizers to be parabolic.
- **C26** (theorem, [source](#stmt-pret-parabolic-star)): Let $\gamma$ be a parabolic element with unique fixed point $a\in P$. Every point of $\Phi$ fixed by $\gamma$ is either $a$ or adjacent to $a$. Consequently, if an infinite-order element fixes two points of $\Phi$ whose interval is infinite, the element is loxodromic and both points are terminal.
  - Explanation: Restricts parabolic fixed points in the pretree completion to the fixed cut point and its neighbors.
- **C27** (theorem, [source](#stmt-pret-invariant-class)): Under the assumptions of this section, $\Gamma$ preserves a finite-interval class of $\Phi$.
  - Explanation: Extracts an invariant finite-interval class through the ordinal collapse argument.
- **C28** (construction, [source](#stmt-pret-tree)): Let $\Gamma$ be a finitely presented torsion-free one-ended group with no nontrivial splitting over a finite or two-ended subgroup. Suppose it acts minimally by convergence on a nondegenerate compact metric continuum $B$ whose set $P$ of global cut points is nonempty. Then there is a nontrivial minimal simplicial $\Gamma$-tree $\mathcal T$, with no edge inversions and finite quotient, such that:* 1. *$P$ is an invariant subset of its vertices, and every edge has one endpoint in $P$ and one outside $P$;* 2. *$\mathcal T$ is spanned by $P$; its other vertices have degree at least two;* 3. *every edge stabilizer is infinite;* 4. *if $a,b,q\in P$ and $q$ separates $a,b$ in $B$, then $q\in[a,b]_{\mathcal T}$.* *Every point of $P$ is fixed by an infinite-order parabolic element.
  - Explanation: Produces the minimal simplicial tree that records separation by cut points.
- **C29** (construction, [source](#stmt-block-graph)): There is a connected $\Gamma$-graph $K^*$ with vertex set $P$ and finitely many edge orbits, such that the endpoints of each edge have distance two in $\mathcal T$. The graph $K$ induced by $K^*$ on $R$ is connected and has finitely many $H$-orbits of vertices and edges.* *Define $\pi:P\to R$ by letting $\pi(p)$ be the neighbor of $r$ on the tree interval from $r$ to $p$. An edge of $K^*$ with endpoints in $R$ is fixed by $\pi$, and every other edge is collapsed to a vertex. Moreover, the stabilizer in $H$ of every vertex of $K$ is infinite.
  - Explanation: Builds an equivariant graph on cut points and the connected graph on a block star.
- **C30** (theorem, [source](#stmt-block-near-path)): If $a_n,b_n\in P$ both tend to $x\in B$, there are paths in $K^*$ from $a_n$ to $b_n$ all of whose vertices tend uniformly to $x$.* *If the projections of the endpoints of a path in $K^*$ are distinct, then, after stationary steps are removed, its projected path uses only vertices of the original path.
  - Explanation: Transfers near-boundary paths to the cut-point graph while controlling projection vertices.
- **C31** (construction, [source](#stmt-block-retraction)): The map $\pi:P\to R$ extends to a continuous retraction $$\mathfrak r:B\longrightarrow L, \qquad L=\overline R^{\,B},$$ which is locally constant on $B\setminus L$. The space $L$ is a nondegenerate continuum without cut points, and $$\dim L\leq1, \qquad \check H^1(L;\mathbb F_2)=0.$$
  - Explanation: Extends the tree projection continuously and transfers dimension and cohomology to a block.
- **C32** (construction, [source](#stmt-block-setting)): There are a subgroup $H\leq\Gamma$, a compact space $Y=H\sqcup L\subset X$, and a connected $H$-graph $K$ with vertex set $I\subset Y$, with the following properties.* 1. *$Y=\overline H^{\,X}$. The points of $H$ are isolated in $Y$, and $L$ is a nondegenerate continuum without cut points satisfying $$\dim L\leq1, \qquad \check H^1(L;\mathbb F_2)=0.$$* 2. *If $h_n\to a\in L$ and $h_n^{-1}\to b\in L$, then $h_n$ converges to the constant map $a$, uniformly on compact subsets of $Y\setminus\{b\}$.* 3. *The graph $K$ has finitely many $H$-orbits of vertices and edges. Either $I=H$, or $I\subset L$. Every point of $L$ is the limit of a sequence of distinct vertices in $I$.* 4. *For each fixed $w\in I$, $$\label{block:comparison} \rho(hw,h)\longrightarrow0 \quad\text{as }h\longrightarrow\infty\text{ in }H.$$* 5. *For every $x\in L$ and every neighborhood $W$ of $x$ in $Y$, there is a neighborhood $V$ of $x$ in $Y$ such that any two vertices of $I\cap V$ can be joined by a path in $K$ all of whose vertices belong to $W$.* 6. *If $I\subset L$, then for each $p\in I$ there is an infinite-order element $h\in H_p$ such that $$h^jz\longrightarrow p \quad(j\longrightarrow+\infty),\qquad z\in L.$$* *No finite generation assumption on $H$ or local finiteness assumption on $K$ is included in these conclusions.
  - Explanation: Collects the compact boundary and equivariant graph interfaces used for path pushing.
- **C33** (theorem, [source](#stmt-ann-extension)): Let $L$ be a nondegenerate compact metric continuum with no cut point and $\check H^1(L;k)=0$. If $x\in L$ and $V$ is an open neighborhood of $x$ in $L$, every locally constant map $u:V\setminus\{x\}\to k$ extends uniquely to a locally constant map on $V$.
  - Explanation: Extends locally constant labels across a deleted point using cohomology and absence of cut points.
- **C34** (theorem, [source](#stmt-ann-component)): In the setting above, each $x\in\Omega_T$ determines a unique component $Q_T(x)$ of $K[T]$ such that all allowed vertices in some neighborhood of $x$ belong to $Q_T(x)$. Every $x\in\Omega_T$ is a limit of distinct allowed vertices, and the assignment $Q_T$ is locally constant on $\Omega_T$. If $t\in T\cap L\cap\Omega_T$, then $Q_T(t)$ is the component containing the vertex $t$ itself.
  - Explanation: Assigns a unique locally constant component label where deleted vertices do not accumulate.
- **C35** (construction, [source](#stmt-ann-system)): There is a set $\mathcal A$ of annuli invariant under $H$ and under interchange of the two sides, with the following properties.* 1. *For every $\delta>0$, only finitely many annuli in $\mathcal A$ have both sides of diameter at least $\delta$.* 2. *For every closed $F\subset Y$, every $x\in L\setminus F$, and every integer $n\geq1$, there is a chain of $n$ annuli in $\mathcal A$ from $F$ to a neighborhood of $x$ in $Y$.
  - Explanation: Constructs invariant annuli with finite large-side counts and arbitrarily long separating chains.
- **C36** (theorem, [source](#stmt-ann-depth)): For $g\in H$, $F\subset Y$, and $v\in Y$, $$D(gF,gv)=D(F,v).$$ If $F$ contains a point of $H$ and $v\in H$, then $D(F,v)$ is finite. When it is positive, a chain attains this finite maximum. If $F$ is closed, $x\in L\setminus F$, and $N\geq1$, there is a neighborhood $V_N$ of $x$ in $Y$ such that $D(F,v)\geq N$ for every $v\in V_N$.
  - Explanation: Establishes equivariance, finiteness on group vertices and depth near boundary points.
- **C37** (theorem, [source](#stmt-push-shrinking)): Let $g_n\in H$ satisfy $D(A,g_n)\to\infty$, and set $F_n=g_n^{-1}A$. After passage to a subsequence there is $q\in L$ such that $F_n$ converges in the Hausdorff metric to $\{q\}$.
  - Explanation: Shrinks translates of the closed set to a boundary singleton along a subsequence.
- **C38** (bound, [source](#stmt-push-depth-comparison)): Suppose $F_n=g_n^{-1}A$ converges to $\{q\}$ as in Lemma <a href="#push:shrinking" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="push:shrinking">37</a>. For each $r>0$, set $$C_r=N(r/2,d_0)+N(d_0,r)+2.$$ For all sufficiently large $n$, uniformly over every $b\in H$ with $\rho(b,q)\ge3r$, one has $$\label{eq:push-depth-comparison} D(F_n,b)\ge D(F_n,1)+D(\{1\},b)-C_r.$$ Consequently, if $b_n\in H$ tends to $z\in L\setminus\{q\}$, then $$D(F_n,b_n)\ge D(F_n,1)+D(\{1\},b_n)-C \quad\hbox{and}\quad D(\{1\},b_n)\longrightarrow\infty$$ for a constant $C$ independent of $n$.
  - Explanation: Concatenates annular chains after bounded pruning to compare depths uniformly.
- **C39** (theorem, [source](#stmt-push-component-labels)): For each fixed $m$, the deleted vertices $I\setminus I_m$ have no accumulation by distinct vertices at any point of $L\setminus A$. Consequently Lemma <a href="#ann:component" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="ann:component">34</a> defines a locally constant map $$Q_m:L\setminus A\longrightarrow \{\text{components of }K[I_m]\},$$ whose value at $x$ contains all vertices of $I_m$ sufficiently near $x$. Every $x\in L\setminus A$ is approached by distinct vertices with this label. Moreover, for each finite subset $E\subset I$ one has $E\cap I_m=\varnothing$ for all sufficiently large $m$.
  - Explanation: Controls deleted-vertex accumulation and defines deep component labels.
- **C40** (theorem, [source](#stmt-push-translated-deletions)): Let $m_n\to\infty$ and $D(A,g_n)\ge m_n$. Pass to a subsequence for which $F_n=g_n^{-1}A$ tends to $\{q\}$, with $q\in L$. For every compact $E\subset Y\setminus\{q\}$ there are a finite set $S_E\subset I$ and an index $n_E$ such that $$E\cap g_n^{-1}(I\setminus I_{m_n+1})\subset S_E \qquad(n\ge n_E).$$
  - Explanation: Bounds translated deletions on compact sets away from the exceptional boundary point.
- **C41** (theorem, [source](#stmt-push-eventual-labels)): For the sequences in Lemma <a href="#push:translated-deletions" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="push:translated-deletions">40</a>, define $$\ell_n(z)=Q_{m_n+1}(g_nz)$$ whenever $z\in L$ and $g_nz\notin A$. For each $z\in L\setminus\{q\}$ there are a relative neighborhood $V_z\subset L\setminus\{q\}$ and an index $n_z$ such that all values $\ell_n(w)$ with $w\in V_z$ and $n\ge n_z$ are defined and $$\ell_n(w)=\ell_n(z)\qquad(w\in V_z,\ n\ge n_z).$$ For any two points $z,z'\in L\setminus\{q\}$ one consequently has $\ell_n(z)=\ell_n(z')$ for all sufficiently large $n$. The last threshold may depend on the two points.
  - Explanation: Makes translated component labels eventually equal for each fixed pair away from the exceptional point.
- **C42** (theorem, [source](#stmt-push-majority)): For every finite set $S\subset H$, there is an integer $M=M(A,Z,S)\ge1$ with the following properties for all integers $m\ge M$.* 1. *If $D(A,g)\ge m$, at least two points of $gZ$ lie in $L\setminus A$ and have a common $Q_{m+1}$ label. Denote this unique majority label by $J_m(g)$.* 2. *If $s\in S$ and $D(A,g),D(A,gs)\ge m$, then $J_m(g)=J_m(gs)$.
  - Explanation: Obtains a common depth threshold for majority labels and a finite collection of moves.
- **C43** (theorem, [source](#stmt-push-refinement)): For every $m\ge M$ and all $x,y\in L\setminus A$, $$\label{eq:push-refinement} Q_m(x)=Q_m(y)\quad\Longrightarrow\quad Q_{m+1}(x)=Q_{m+1}(y).$$ In particular, the threshold $M$ is independent of $x$ and $y$.
  - Explanation: Preserves component equality at every deeper level beyond a uniform threshold.
- **C44** (theorem, [source](#stmt-push-local-connected)): In the setting of Proposition <a href="#block:setting" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="block:setting">32</a>, the continuum $L$ is locally connected.
  - Explanation: Obtains local connectivity from Hausdorff limits of paths whose edge mesh vanishes.
- **C45** (theorem, [source](#stmt-end-restriction)): If $Z$ is a compact metric space with $\dim Z\le1$, $A\subset Z$ is closed, and $k$ is an abelian coefficient group, then restriction induces a surjection $$\check H^1(Z;k)\longrightarrow\check H^1(A;k).$$
  - Explanation: Extends closed-subset cohomology classes using a refinement whose nerve is a graph.
- **C46** (theorem, [source](#stmt-end-paths-in-regions)): Let $L$ be a locally connected compact metric space with metric $d$, and let $U\subset L$ be connected and open. Any two points $a,b\in U$ are joined by a continuous path in $U$.
  - Explanation: Constructs paths inside connected open regions through successively finer chains.
- **C47** (theorem, [source](#stmt-end-continuum)): Every nondegenerate locally connected compact metric continuum $L$ with $$\dim L\le1,\qquad \check H^1(L;\mathbb F_2)=0$$ has a cut point.
  - Explanation: Deduces a cut point from local connectivity, the dimension bound and vanishing cohomology.
- **C48** (theorem, [source](#stmt-cor-euler)): Let $Q$ be a finite connected aspherical integral Poincaré complex of formal dimension four, with arbitrary orientation character. Then $$\chi(Q)=b_2^{(2)}(\widetilde Q)\ge0.$$ Moreover, $\chi(Q)=0$ if and only if every $L^2$-Betti number of $\widetilde Q$ vanishes. The same conclusions hold for closed connected aspherical topological four-manifolds.
  - Explanation: Identifies Euler characteristic with the remaining middle-degree invariant.
- **C49** (bound, [source](#stmt-cor-signature)): Every closed connected oriented aspherical topological four-manifold satisfies $$\chi(M)\ge \|\sigma(M)\|.$$ In particular, $\chi(M)=0$ implies $\sigma(M)=0$.
  - Explanation: Bounds the absolute signature using the topological lifted intersection form.
- **C50** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-cor-betti-growth)): Let $Q$ be as in Corollary <a href="#cor:euler" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="cor:euler">48</a>, and suppose that $\Gamma=\pi_1(Q)$ admits a descending sequence $\Gamma=\Gamma_0\supseteq\Gamma_1\supseteq\cdots$ of finite-index normal subgroups with $\bigcap_i\Gamma_i=\{1\}$. Let $Q_i\to Q$ be the associated covers. For every $p\ge0$, $$\lim_{i\to\infty} \frac{b_p(Q_i;\mathbb Q)}{[\Gamma:\Gamma_i]} =\begin{cases} \chi(Q),&p=2,\\ 0,&p\ne2. \end{cases}$$ The same statement holds for a closed connected aspherical topological four-manifold and its corresponding covers.
  - Explanation: Determines normalized rational Betti limits along the specified normal cover tower.

        "exact_quote": "For every base coordinate $F$ and $g\\in\\Gamma$, $$\\int_0^1\\sum_{e\\in S(F,t)}\\sum_{e'\\text{ near }e}\n       \\lambda(ge')\\,dt\n =\\sum_e |dF(e)|\\sum_{e'\\text{ near }e}\\lambda(ge').\n \\label{eq:dyn-length}$$ This quantity is finite and tends to zero as $g\\to\\infty$.       },

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-pret-item-fixed",
        "exact_quote": " Let a group act minimally by convergence on a nondegenerate compact metric cont",
        "source_id": "canonical:PM-315.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-pret-item-fixed",
          "exact_quote": " Let a group act minimally by convergence on a nondegenerate compact metric cont",
          "source_id": "canonical:PM-315.1"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "<span id=\"pret:package\" label=\"pret:package\"></span> Let a group act minimally by convergence on a nondegenerate compact metric continuum $B$, and let $P\\ne\\varnothing$ be its set of global cut points. There is a complete median pretree $\\Phi$ containing $P$ equivariantly, with the same betweenness on $P$, with these properties.*\n\n1.  *<span id=\"pret:item-adjacency\" label=\"pret:item-adjacency\"></span> Two distinct points are called adjacent if their open interval is empty. Every adjacent pair in $\\Phi$ has one point in $P$ and one outside $P$. A full subset whose intervals are finite is the vertex set of a simplicial tree, with these adjacent pairs as edges.*\n\n\n2.  *<span id=\"pret:item-support\" label=\"pret:item-support\"></span> There is an equivariant map $\\phi:B\\to\\Phi$, equal to the identity on $P$, and, for every $q\\in\\Phi$, an equivariantly assigned nonempty compact subset $R_B(q)\\subset B$. For $q\\in P$ this set is $\\{q\\}$; for $q\\notin P$ it satisfies $$\\label{pret:support}\n     R_B(q)=\\phi^{-1}(q)\\cup\\Lambda(q),\n     \\qquad \\Lambda(q)=\\{p\\in P:p\\text{ is adjacent to }q\\}.$$ No point of $\\Phi$ is fixed by the whole group.*\n\n3.  *<span id=\"pret:item-quotient\" label=\"pret:item-quotient\"></span> Starting with equality on $\\Phi$, successively collapse finite-interval classes (two points belong to one such class when their closed interval in the current quotient is finite), and at limit ordinals take the union of the preceding equivalence relations. This process stabilizes. Its final quotient is the canonical dendritic quotient $D(B)$ of $B$. In particular, $D(B)$ is a dendrite and there is a continuous equivariant surjection $B\\to D(B)$. The induced action on $D(B)$ is minimal and convergence.*\n\n4.  *If a loxodromic element fixes $q,r\\in\\Phi$ with $[q,r]$ infinite, then $q,r$ are terminal in $\\Phi$: neither lies strictly between two points. If a parabolic element has its unique fixed point in $B\\setminus P$, then it has exactly one fixed point in $\\Phi$.",
      "explanation": "Packages the cut-point pretree, compact supports and successive interval collapses.",
      "fidelity": "PENDING",
      "hypotheses": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-pret-item-fixed",
          "exact_quote": " Let a group act minimally by convergence on a nondegenerate compact metric cont",
          "source_id": "canonical:PM-315.1"
        }
      ],
      "id": "C24",
      "kind": "construction",
      "quantifiers": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-pret-item-fixed",
          "exact_quote": " Let a group act minimally by convergence on a nondegenerate compact metric cont",
          "source_id": "canonical:PM-315.1"
        }
      ],
      "statement_anchor": "stmt-pret-item-fixed"
    },

        }

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-pret-tree",
        "exact_quote": "Let $\\Gamma$ be a finitely presented torsion-free one-ended group with no nontrivial splitting over a finite or two-ended subgroup. Suppose it acts minimally by convergence on a nondegenerate compact metric continuum $B$ whose set $P$ of global cut points is nonempty. Then there is a nontrivial minimal simplicial $\\Gamma$-tree $\\mathcal T$, with no edge inversions and finite quotient, such that:*\n\n1.  *$P$ is an invariant subset of its vertices, and every edge has one endpoint in $P$ and one outside $P$;*\n\n2.  *$\\mathcal T$ is spanned by $P$; its other vertices have degree at least two;*\n\n3.  *every edge stabilizer is infinite;*\n\n4.  *if $a,b,q\\in P$ and $q$ separates $a,b$ in $B$, then $q\\in[a,b]_{\\mathcal T}$.*\n\n*Every point of $P$ is fixed by an infinite-order parabolic element.",
        "source_id": "canonical:PM-315.1"
      },

        "anchor": "stmt-block-setting",
        "exact_quote": "There are a subgroup $H\\leq\\Gamma$, a compact space $Y=H\\sqcup L\\subset X$, and a connected $H$-graph $K$ with vertex set $I\\subset Y$, with the following properties.*\n\n1.",

      "exact_statement": "There are a subgroup $H\\leq\\Gamma$, a compact space $Y=H\\sqcup L\\subset X$, and a connected $H$-graph $K$ with vertex set $I\\subset Y$, with the following properties.*\n\n1.  *$Y=\\overline H^{\\,X}$. The points of $H$ are isolated in $Y$, and $L$ is a nondegenerate continuum without cut points satisfying $$\\dim L\\leq1,\n      \\qquad \\check H^1(L;\\mathbb F_2)=0.$$*\n\n2.  *If $h_n\\to a\\in L$ and $h_n^{-1}\\to b\\in L$, then $h_n$ converges to the constant map $a$, uniformly on compact subsets of $Y\\setminus\\{b\\}$.*\n\n3.  *The graph $K$ has finitely many $H$-orbits of vertices and edges. Either $I=H$, or $I\\subset L$. Every point of $L$ is the limit of a sequence of distinct vertices in $I$.*\n\n\n4.  *For each fixed $w\\in I$, $$\\label{block:comparison}\n      \\rho(hw,h)\\longrightarrow0\n      \\quad\\text{as }h\\longrightarrow\\infty\\text{ in }H.$$*\n\n5.  *For every $x\\in L$ and every neighborhood $W$ of $x$ in $Y$, there is a neighborhood $V$ of $x$ in $Y$ such that any two vertices of $I\\cap V$ can be joined by a path in $K$ all of whose vertices belong to $W$.*\n\n6.  *If $I\\subset L$, then for each $p\\in I$ there is an infinite-order element $h\\in H_p$ such that $$h^jz\\longrightarrow p\n      \\quad(j\\longrightarrow+\\infty),\\qquad z\\in L.$$*\n\n*No finite generation assumption on $H$ or local finiteness assumption on $K$ is included in these conclusions.",

          "exact_quote": "There is a set $\\mathcal A$ of annuli invariant under $H$ and under interchange of the two sides, with the following properties.*\n\n1.  *For every $\\delta>0$, only finitely many annuli in $\\mathcal A$ have both sides of diameter at least $\\delta$.*\n\n2.  *For every closed $F\\subset Y$, every $x\\in L\\setminus F$, and every integer $n\\geq1$, there is a chain of $n$ annuli in $\\mathcal A$ from $F$ to a neighborhood of $x$ in $Y$.",
          "source_id": "canonical:PM-315.1"
        }
      ],

        "anchor": "stmt-ann-depth",
        "exact_quote": "For $g\\in H$, $F\\subset Y$, and $v\\in Y$, $$D(gF,gv)=D(F,v).$$ If $F$ contains a point of $H$ and $v\\in H$, then $D(F,v)$ is finite.       "exact_statement": "Suppose $F_n=g_n^{-1}A$ converges to $\\{q\\}$ as in Lemma <a href=\"#push:shrinking\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"push:shrinking\">37</a>. <a id="pm-s2"></a>
## 2. Definitions, validity and bounds

- **aspherical** ([source](#sec:introduction)): A connected CW complex or manifold is *aspherical* if its universal cover is contractible.
- **closed manifold** ([source](#sec:introduction)): Throughout, a closed manifold is compact and has no boundary.
- **integral Poincaré complex** ([source](#sec:introduction)): A *finite integral Poincaré complex of formal dimension four* means a finite connected CW complex $Q$ equipped with an orientation character $w:\pi_1(Q)\to\{1,-1\}$ and a fundamental class $[Q]\in H_4(Q;\mathbb Z^w)$ for which cap product gives duality in every degree with every local coefficient system, with the orientation twist on homology.
- **group von Neumann algebra and dimension** ([source](#sec:introduction)): For a discrete group $\Gamma$, write $\mathcal N\Gamma$ for its group von Neumann algebra and $\dim_{\mathcal N\Gamma}$ for the extended Murray–von Neumann dimension.
- **singular-chain invariant** ([source](#sec:introduction)): If $\widetilde Q\to Q$ is the universal cover, with deck group $\Gamma=\pi_1(Q)$, set $$b_p^{(2)}(\widetilde Q) =\dim_{\mathcal N\Gamma}H_p\bigl( \mathcal N\Gamma\otimes_{\mathbb Z\Gamma}C_*^s(\widetilde Q)\bigr),$$ where $C_*^s$ denotes singular chains.
- **finite energy** ([source](#pm-h-10)): We call $F$ a finite-energy function when $\mathcal E(F)<\infty$.
- **regular level** ([source](#pm-h-10)): A level $t$ is *regular for $F$* if it is not a vertex value.
- **global cut point** ([source](#pm-h-25)): A point $p\in B$ is a *global cut point* if $B\setminus\{p\}$ is disconnected.
- **annulus** ([source](#pm-h-34)): An *annulus* in $Y$ is an ordered pair $\sigma=(\sigma^-,\sigma^+)$ of disjoint closed subsets of $Y$ such that each side meets $L$ and $\sigma^-\cup\sigma^+\ne Y$.
- **annular depth** ([source](#pm-h-34)): For $F\subset Y$ and $v\in Y$, define $$D(F,v)=\sup\{n\geq1: \text{a chain of length }n\text{ in }\mathcal A \text{ goes from }F\text{ to }\{v\}\},$$ with value $0$ if there is no such chain.
- **signature** ([source](#sec:consequences)): For an oriented closed four-manifold $M$, let $\sigma(M)$ be the signature of its real intersection pairing on $H_2(M;\mathbb R)$.

| id | quantity | relation | value | regime |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Q1 | b_p^{(2)}(\widetilde Q) | = | $0$ | All nonnegative degrees outside the middle degree. |
| Q2 | \chi(Q) | = | $b_2^{(2)}(\widetilde Q)$ | Euler characteristic and middle von Neumann dimension. |
| Q3 | \chi(M) | ≥ | $\|\sigma(M)\|$ | Ordinary signature and Euler characteristic. |
| Q4 | \check H^1(B;k) | = | $0$ | Mod-two coefficients. |
| Q5 | \dim L | ≤ | $1$ | Covering dimension. |
| Q6 | coordinate weight | = | $2^{-j}/(1+\\|dF_j\\|_2)$ | Positive coordinate indices. |
| Q7 | edge orbit crossing count | ≤ | $2\|\operatorname{supp}v\|$ | All group elements and the finite-support principal cocycle. |
| Q8 | pruning constant | = | $N(r/2,d_0)+N(d_0,r)+2$ | Positive radius with the specified large-side annulus counts. |
| Q9 | scale parameter | = | $2^{-i}$ | The scale indices. |
| Q10 | Euler characteristic of a product of surfaces | = | $4(g-1)(h-1)$ | The surface genera. |

<a id="pm-s3"></a>
## 3. Proof roadmap

- **R3** [claim](#stmt-found-model) / [proof](#proof-stmt-found-model): Supplies the finite homotopy model and the group properties required by the subsequent reductions. -> Supplies the finite homotopy model and the group properties required by the subsequent reductions.
- **R4** [claim](#stmt-found-comparison) / [proof](#proof-stmt-found-comparison): Identifies algebraic homology dimension with a harmonic Hilbert kernel through chain comparison. -> Identifies algebraic homology dimension with a harmonic Hilbert kernel through chain comparison.
- **R5** [claim](#stmt-found-reduction) / [proof](#proof-stmt-found-reduction): Reduces concentration to vanishing in the first degree, including arbitrary orientation character. -> Reduces concentration to vanishing in the first degree, including arbitrary orientation character.
- **R6** [claim](#stmt-found-duality) / [proof](#proof-stmt-found-duality): Transfers local-coefficient duality to group-ring cohomology and subgroup homology. -> Transfers local-coefficient duality to group-ring cohomology and subgroup homology.
- **R7** [claim](#stmt-found-ends) / [proof](#proof-stmt-found-ends): Excludes multiple ends by correcting a finite-support gradient. -> Excludes multiple ends by correcting a finite-support gradient.
- **R8** [claim](#stmt-found-trees) / [proof](#proof-stmt-found-trees): Obstructs tree actions whose stabilizers have the excluded end types. -> Obstructs tree actions whose stabilizers have the excluded end types.
- **R9** [claim](#stmt-found-gradient) / [proof](#proof-stmt-found-gradient): Converts positive first von Neumann dimension into a proper inclusion of gradient spaces. -> Converts positive first von Neumann dimension into a proper inclusion of gradient spaces.
- **R10** [claim](#stmt-compact-potential) / [proof](#proof-stmt-compact-potential): Produces the bounded energy minimizer with connected regular level sides. -> Produces the bounded energy minimizer with connected regular level sides.
- **R12** [claim](#stmt-compact-good-boxes) / [proof](#proof-stmt-compact-good-boxes): Provides coordinate boxes with finitely many vertex components. -> Provides coordinate boxes with finitely many vertex components.
- **R13** [claim](#stmt-compact-localized-energy) / [proof](#proof-stmt-compact-localized-energy): Preserves finite energy when coordinates record individual box components. -> Preserves finite energy when coordinates record individual box components.
- **R14** [claim](#stmt-compact-boundary) / [proof](#proof-stmt-compact-boundary): Establishes the nondegenerate continuum boundary from one-endedness and finite energy. -> Establishes the nondegenerate continuum boundary from one-endedness and finite energy.
- **R15** [claim](#stmt-compact-near-path) / [proof](#proof-stmt-compact-near-path): Connects vertices near the same boundary point by graph paths inside a prescribed neighborhood. -> Connects vertices near the same boundary point by graph paths inside a prescribed neighborhood.
- **R18** [claim](#stmt-dyn-convergence) / [proof](#proof-stmt-dyn-convergence): Obtains compact-uniform convergence away from the repelling point and proper discontinuity on triples. -> Obtains compact-uniform convergence away from the repelling point and proper discontinuity on triples.
- **R20** [claim](#stmt-dyn-minimal) / [proof](#proof-stmt-dyn-minimal): Excludes a global fixed point and establishes orbit density. -> Excludes a global fixed point and establishes orbit density.
- **R21** [claim](#stmt-topo-thin-walls) / [proof](#proof-stmt-topo-thin-walls): Controls boundary accumulation of regular cuts by vanishing Hausdorff measure. -> Controls boundary accumulation of regular cuts by vanishing Hausdorff measure.
- **R22** [claim](#stmt-topo-dimension) / [proof](#proof-stmt-topo-dimension): Constructs arbitrarily fine covers with bounded multiplicity to control boundary dimension. -> Constructs arbitrarily fine covers with bounded multiplicity to control boundary dimension.
- **R23** [claim](#stmt-topo-cech) / [proof](#proof-stmt-topo-cech): Kills boundary cover cocycles through a finite-support cellular correction. -> Kills boundary cover cocycles through a finite-support cellular correction.
- **R25** [claim](#stmt-pret-parabolic-cuts) / [proof](#proof-stmt-pret-parabolic-cuts): Forces the dendritic quotient to be trivial and cut-point stabilizers to be parabolic. -> Forces the dendritic quotient to be trivial and cut-point stabilizers to be parabolic.
- **R26** [claim](#stmt-pret-parabolic-star) / [proof](#proof-stmt-pret-parabolic-star): Restricts parabolic fixed points in the pretree completion to the fixed cut point and its neighbors. -> Restricts parabolic fixed points in the pretree completion to the fixed cut point and its neighbors.
- **R27** [claim](#stmt-pret-invariant-class) / [proof](#proof-stmt-pret-invariant-class): Extracts an invariant finite-interval class through the ordinal collapse argument. -> Extracts an invariant finite-interval class through the ordinal collapse argument.
- **R28** [claim](#stmt-pret-tree) / [proof](#proof-stmt-pret-tree): Produces the minimal simplicial tree that records separation by cut points. -> Produces the minimal simplicial tree that records separation by cut points.
- **R29** [claim](#stmt-block-graph) / [proof](#proof-stmt-block-graph): Builds an equivariant graph on cut points and the connected graph on a block star. -> Builds an equivariant graph on cut points and the connected graph on a block star.
- **R30** [claim](#stmt-block-near-path) / [proof](#proof-stmt-block-near-path): Transfers near-boundary paths to the cut-point graph while controlling projection vertices. -> Transfers near-boundary paths to the cut-point graph while controlling projection vertices.
- **R31** [claim](#stmt-block-retraction) / [proof](#proof-stmt-block-retraction): Extends the tree projection continuously and transfers dimension and cohomology to a block. -> Extends the tree projection continuously and transfers dimension and cohomology to a block.
- **R32** [claim](#stmt-block-setting) / [proof](#proof-stmt-block-setting): Collects the compact boundary and equivariant graph interfaces used for path pushing. -> Collects the compact boundary and equivariant graph interfaces used for path pushing.
- **R33** [claim](#stmt-ann-extension) / [proof](#proof-stmt-ann-extension): Extends locally constant labels across a deleted point using cohomology and absence of cut points. -> Extends locally constant labels across a deleted point using cohomology and absence of cut points.
- **R34** [claim](#stmt-ann-component) / [proof](#proof-stmt-ann-component): Assigns a unique locally constant component label where deleted vertices do not accumulate. -> Assigns a unique locally constant component label where deleted vertices do not accumulate.
- **R35** [claim](#stmt-ann-system) / [proof](#proof-stmt-ann-system): Constructs invariant annuli with finite large-side counts and arbitrarily long separating chains. -> Constructs invariant annuli with finite large-side counts and arbitrarily long separating chains.
- **R36** [claim](#stmt-ann-depth) / [proof](#proof-stmt-ann-depth): Establishes equivariance, finiteness on group vertices and depth near boundary points. -> Establishes equivariance, finiteness on group vertices and depth near boundary points.
- **R37** [claim](#stmt-push-shrinking) / [proof](#proof-stmt-push-shrinking): Shrinks translates of the closed set to a boundary singleton along a subsequence. -> Shrinks translates of the closed set to a boundary singleton along a subsequence.
- **R38** [claim](#stmt-push-depth-comparison) / [proof](#proof-stmt-push-depth-comparison): Concatenates annular chains after bounded pruning to compare depths uniformly. -> Concatenates annular chains after bounded pruning to compare depths uniformly.
- **R39** [claim](#stmt-push-component-labels) / [proof](#proof-stmt-push-component-labels): Controls deleted-vertex accumulation and defines deep component labels. -> Controls deleted-vertex accumulation and defines deep component labels.
- **R40** [claim](#stmt-push-translated-deletions) / [proof](#proof-stmt-push-translated-deletions): Bounds translated deletions on compact sets away from the exceptional boundary point. -> Bounds translated deletions on compact sets away from the exceptional boundary point.
- **R41** [claim](#stmt-push-eventual-labels) / [proof](#proof-stmt-push-eventual-labels): Makes translated component labels eventually equal for each fixed pair away from the exceptional point. -> Makes translated component labels eventually equal for each fixed pair away from the exceptional point.
- **R42** [claim](#stmt-push-majority) / [proof](#proof-stmt-push-majority): Obtains a common depth threshold for majority labels and a finite collection of moves. -> Obtains a common depth threshold for majority labels and a finite collection of moves.
- **R43** [claim](#stmt-push-refinement) / [proof](#proof-stmt-push-refinement): Preserves component equality at every deeper level beyond a uniform threshold. -> Preserves component equality at every deeper level beyond a uniform threshold.
- **R44** [claim](#stmt-push-local-connected) / [proof](#proof-stmt-push-local-connected): Obtains local connectivity from Hausdorff limits of paths whose edge mesh vanishes. -> Obtains local connectivity from Hausdorff limits of paths whose edge mesh vanishes.
- **R45** [claim](#stmt-end-restriction) / [proof](#proof-stmt-end-restriction): Extends closed-subset cohomology classes using a refinement whose nerve is a graph. -> Extends closed-subset cohomology classes using a refinement whose nerve is a graph.
- **R46** [claim](#stmt-end-paths-in-regions) / [proof](#proof-stmt-end-paths-in-regions): Constructs paths inside connected open regions through successively finer chains. -> Constructs paths inside connected open regions through successively finer chains.
- **R47** [claim](#stmt-end-continuum) / [proof](#proof-stmt-end-continuum): Deduces a cut point from local connectivity, the dimension bound and vanishing cohomology. -> Deduces a cut point from local connectivity, the dimension bound and vanishing cohomology.
- **R1** [claim](#stmt-thm-main) / [proof](#proof-stmt-end-continuum-2): Establishes concentration of the universal-cover invariants in the middle degree under absolute integral duality. -> Establishes concentration of the universal-cover invariants in the middle degree under absolute integral duality.
- **R2** [claim](#stmt-cor-singer-manifolds) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cor-singer-manifolds): Transfers the concentration theorem to closed aspherical topological manifolds by a finite homotopy model. -> Transfers the concentration theorem to closed aspherical topological manifolds by a finite homotopy model.
- **R48** [claim](#stmt-cor-euler) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cor-euler): Identifies Euler characteristic with the remaining middle-degree invariant. -> Identifies Euler characteristic with the remaining middle-degree invariant.
- **R49** [claim](#stmt-cor-signature) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cor-signature): Bounds the absolute signature using the topological lifted intersection form. -> Bounds the absolute signature using the topological lifted intersection form.
- **R50** [claim](#stmt-cor-betti-growth) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cor-betti-growth): Determines normalized rational Betti limits along the specified normal cover tower. -> Determines normalized rational Betti limits along the specified normal cover tower.

        "Let $\\Lambda$ be a group and let $P_*$ be a free $\\mathbb Z\\Lambda$-resolution of $\\mathbb Z$ whose terms in degrees $0,1,2$ are finitely generated. Suppose that a contractible space $Z$ has a free $\\Lambda$-action for which its singular chains are free $\\mathbb Z\\Lambda$-modules. Then $$b_1^{(2)}(Z;\\mathcal N(\\Lambda))\n =\\dim_{\\mathcal N(\\Lambda)}\n   \\bigl(\\ker\\partial_1\\cap\\ker\\partial_2^*\\bigr),$$ where the operators on the right act on the finite Hilbert modules $\\ell^2(\\Lambda)\\otimes_{\\mathbb Z\\Lambda}P_i$. The same space represents first reduced Hilbert cohomology for $d_0=\\partial_1^*$ and $d_1=\\partial_2^*$. The analogous comparison holds in every degree of a finite free cellular complex."
      ],

<a id="pm-s4"></a>
## 4. Engineering Triptych

### Panel 1 - epistemic contract

- **middle_concentration**: The universal-cover $L^2$-Betti numbers vanish outside degree two.
- **euler_nonnegative**: The Euler characteristic equals the remaining middle $L^2$-Betti number and is nonnegative.
- **signature_constraint**: The Euler characteristic bounds the absolute signature.
- **cover_limit**: Normalized rational Betti numbers in the specified cover tower tend to the Euler characteristic in degree two and to zero in every other degree.
- **boundary_connectivity**: The boundary block is locally connected only after the uniform path-pushing argument.

### Panel 2 - negative knowledge

- **finite_graph_certificate**: Use homology of a finite sampled graph as a certificate for universal-cover $L^2$ vanishing. -> A finite sample does not identify the von Neumann dimension of the infinite-cover homology.
- **middle_vanishing**: Conclude that every $L^2$-Betti number vanishes. -> The middle degree can remain positive.
- **arbitrary_tower**: Infer the asserted normalized Betti-number limit from an arbitrary sequence of finite covers. -> The approximation consequence is stated for a descending normal tower with trivial intersection.
- **signature_extension**: Apply the signature inequality to every finite Poincaré complex. -> The signature consequence uses an additional topological manifold signature theorem.
- **boundary_shortcut**: Replace the path-pushing proof by local finiteness of the auxiliary graph or by assumed local connectivity of the boundary. -> Neither graph local finiteness nor boundary local connectivity is available as an initial input.

### Panel 3 - reference implementation and oracle

Applicability: `not_applicable`. The proof constructs a boundary from a countable family of finite-energy functions and establishes local connectedness through paths at arbitrarily large annular depth and Hausdorff limits. These infinite constructions do not supply a finite input representation, a terminating procedure, or a computable depth threshold. A finite model of a graph or a cover cannot certify the universal-cover vanishing theorem.

<a id="pm-s6"></a>
## 6. Applicability limits and search

- **L1** (hypothesis): The concentration theorem requires a finite connected aspherical integral Poincaré complex of formal dimension four. Its conclusion is conditional on these hypotheses. Blocked use: ['Applying the theorem to an arbitrary finite complex or a nonaspherical manifold.']
- **L2** (hypothesis): The duality assumption is absolute duality with every local coefficient system, including the orientation twist on homology. Blocked use: ['Substituting duality for a Poincaré pair or untwisted ordinary homology alone.']
- **L3** (edge_case): Orientability, a geometric structure, smoothability, and triangulability are not prerequisites for the topological manifold conclusion. A finite complex supplied for such a manifold is a homotopy model. Blocked use: ['Rejecting a topological manifold solely because it has no smooth structure or triangulation.', 'Treating the homotopy model as a triangulation of the manifold.']
- **L4** (counterexample_scope): Concentration outside the middle degree does not imply vanishing in the middle degree. Blocked use: ['Inferring that every admissible universal cover has all its $L^2$-Betti numbers zero.']

<a id="pm-s7"></a>
## 7. Complete mathematical paper


We prove the four-dimensional Singer conjecture: the <i>L</i><sup>2</sup>-Betti numbers of the universal cover of a closed connected aspherical topological four-manifold vanish outside degree two. More generally, the same vanishing holds for finite connected aspherical integral Poincaré complexes of formal dimension four, including nonorientable ones.

<a id="sec:introduction"></a>
### Introduction

The Singer conjecture predicts that the $L^2$-homology of the universal cover of a closed aspherical manifold is concentrated in its middle dimension. In dimension four, Poincaré duality reduces the problem to vanishing in degree one. The conclusion forces the Euler characteristic to be nonnegative. We prove this vanishing for closed aspherical topological four-manifolds and, more generally, for finite aspherical integral Poincaré complexes of formal dimension four.

A connected CW complex or manifold is *aspherical* if its universal cover is contractible. Throughout, a closed manifold is compact and has no boundary. A *finite integral Poincaré complex of formal dimension four* means a finite connected CW complex $Q$ equipped with an orientation character $w:\pi_1(Q)\to\{1,-1\}$ and a fundamental class $[Q]\in H_4(Q;\mathbb Z^w)$ for which cap product gives duality in every degree with every local coefficient system, with the orientation twist on homology. This is absolute duality for $Q$, not duality for a Poincaré pair. Equivalently, the duality condition can be tested on the regular group-ring local system (Wall 1967, Lemma 1.1) <a id="pm-cite-1"></a>[[Wall1967](#ref-Wall1967)].

For a discrete group $\Gamma$, write $\mathcal N\Gamma$ for its group von Neumann algebra and $\dim_{\mathcal N\Gamma}$ for the extended Murray–von Neumann dimension. If $\widetilde Q\to Q$ is the universal cover, with deck group $\Gamma=\pi_1(Q)$, set $$b_p^{(2)}(\widetilde Q)
 =\dim_{\mathcal N\Gamma}H_p\bigl(
 \mathcal N\Gamma\otimes_{\mathbb Z\Gamma}C_*^s(\widetilde Q)\bigr),$$ where $C_*^s$ denotes singular chains. This definition applies to CW complexes and topological manifolds and agrees with the usual cellular definition (Lück 2002, Definition 6.50 and Lemmas 6.51–6.53) <a id="pm-cite-2"></a>[[Luck2002](#ref-Luck2002)].

<a id="stmt-thm-main"></a>
<div id="thm:main" class="theorem">

**Theorem 1**. *Let $Q$ be a finite connected aspherical integral Poincaré complex of formal dimension four, with arbitrary orientation character. Then $$b_p^{(2)}(\widetilde Q)=0\qquad(p\ge0,\ p\ne2).$$*

</div>

<a id="stmt-cor-singer-manifolds"></a>
<div id="cor:singer-manifolds" class="corollary">

**Corollary 2**. *Let $M$ be a closed connected aspherical topological four-manifold. Then $$b_p^{(2)}(\widetilde M)=0\qquad(p\ge0,\ p\ne2).$$*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-cor-singer-manifolds"><summary>Proof of stmt-cor-singer-manifolds</summary>

*Proof.* Lemma <a href="#found:model" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="found:model">3</a> supplies a finite aspherical integral Poincaré-complex homotopy model of $M$. A homotopy equivalence lifts to an equivariant homotopy equivalence of universal covers and preserves the singular-chain invariants above. Theorem <a href="#thm:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:main">1</a> applies to the model. ◻

</details>

Corollary <a href="#cor:singer-manifolds" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="cor:singer-manifolds">2</a> establishes the Singer conjecture in dimension four. Neither orientability nor a geometric structure on $M$ is required. The substantive assertion is the vanishing in degree one; Poincaré duality supplies the other nonmiddle degrees. In particular, the corollary includes manifolds that admit no smooth structure or triangulation. The finite complex used for such a manifold is a homotopy model.

For every $Q$ in Theorem <a href="#thm:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:main">1</a>, the remaining invariant is $$\chi(Q)=b_2^{(2)}(\widetilde Q)\ge0.$$ Thus $\chi(Q)=0$ exactly when all its $L^2$-Betti numbers vanish. For an oriented manifold $M$ in Corollary <a href="#cor:singer-manifolds" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="cor:singer-manifolds">2</a>, the $L^2$-signature theorem also gives $\chi(M)\ge|\sigma(M)|$. Along a nested normal tower of finite covers with trivial subgroup intersection, $L^2$ approximation determines the limits of normalized rational Betti numbers. Section <a href="#sec:consequences" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:consequences">11</a> gives the precise statements and proofs of these consequences.

The finite-Poincaré-complex extension was formulated in the earlier literature (Lück 2016, Remark 11.3) <a id="pm-cite-3"></a>[[Luck2016Approximation](#ref-Luck2016Approximation)]. It applies beyond manifold models: the companion *A PD4 group without an aspherical manifold model* constructs a finite oriented aspherical integral Poincaré complex of formal dimension four whose fundamental group is not that of any closed aspherical topological four-manifold (OpenAI 2026a, Theorem 1.1) <a id="pm-cite-4"></a>[[OpenAIPD4Nonmanifold2026](#ref-OpenAIPD4Nonmanifold2026)]. Theorem <a href="#thm:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:main">1</a> applies to that example directly.

<a id="pm-h-3"></a>
#### History and significance

Atiyah’s $L^2$ index theorem explains the connection with Euler characteristic. For an elliptic operator on a closed smooth manifold, its index agrees with the von Neumann index of the lifted operator on a normal cover (Atiyah 1976, Theorem 3.8 and Section 6.4) <a id="pm-cite-5"></a>[[Atiyah1976](#ref-Atiyah1976)]. Applied to differential forms, this identifies the Euler characteristic with the alternating sum of the $L^2$-Betti numbers. Dodziuk’s analytic–simplicial comparison and homotopy-invariance theorem (Dodziuk 1977, Theorems 1–2) <a id="pm-cite-6"></a>[[Dodziuk1977](#ref-Dodziuk1977)] connect this analytic description to topology. The algebraic definition used here also applies to finite complexes and topological manifolds without a smooth structure.

Dodziuk records Singer’s proposal to obtain the Euler-characteristic sign from vanishing of $L^2$-harmonic forms on universal covers (Dodziuk 1979, 396) <a id="pm-cite-7"></a>[[Dodziuk1979](#ref-Dodziuk1979)]. His discussion concerns nonpositive curvature. The later aspherical-manifold formulation asks that, for a closed aspherical $n$-manifold, the $L^2$-Betti numbers vanish whenever $p\ne n/2$; see (Davis and Okun 2001, Conjecture 0.3) <a id="pm-cite-8"></a>[[DavisOkun2001](#ref-DavisOkun2001)] and (Kirstein, Kremer, and Lück 2025, Conjecture 4.1) <a id="pm-cite-9"></a>[[KirsteinKremerLuck2025](#ref-KirsteinKremerLuck2025)]. For even $n=2m$, concentration would give $(-1)^m\chi(M)=b_m^{(2)}(\widetilde M)\ge0$. In dimension four the remaining vanishing question is $b_1^{(2)}=0$: the degree-zero vanishing and Poincaré duality determine the other nonmiddle degrees. Lott and Lück’s work on three-manifolds (Lott and Lück 1995, Theorem 0.1) <a id="pm-cite-10"></a>[[LottLuck1995](#ref-LottLuck1995)] illustrates an earlier route through geometric decomposition. In the closed case, their computation assumed that each prime factor had a finite cover homotopy equivalent to a Haken, Seifert, or hyperbolic manifold; geometrization later removed this restriction for closed aspherical three-manifolds.

Several four-dimensional cases precede the general result. Davis and Okun proved the vanishing for right-angled Coxeter groups whose nerves are flag triangulations of the three-sphere (Davis and Okun 2001, Theorem 11.1.1) <a id="pm-cite-11"></a>[[DavisOkun2001](#ref-DavisOkun2001)] <a id="pm-cite-12"></a>[[OkunSchreve2016](#ref-OkunSchreve2016)]. Okun and Schreve established the Singer conjecture in dimensions at most four for groups of type $\mathrm{VF}$ admitting a hierarchy, and for Coxeter groups whose nerves are arbitrary triangulations of the three-sphere (Okun and Schreve 2016, Theorems 4.16–4.17). Here type $\mathrm{VF}$ means that a finite-index subgroup has a finite classifying space. A hierarchy, in their sense, repeatedly cuts a contractible manifold carrying a proper cocompact group action along invariant locally flat hypersurfaces with contractible components, ending in compact contractible pieces. The Singer conclusion concerns an initial manifold without boundary. These results permit topological manifolds. Their extra input is the specified decomposition, which is not supplied by Poincaré duality alone.

Complex geometry provides another route. The closed aspherical Kähler surface case follows from Gromov’s restriction on Kähler groups with positive first $L^2$-Betti number, as explained in (Albanese, Di Cerbo, and Lombardi 2025, Theorem 4.1) <a id="pm-cite-13"></a>[[AlbaneseDiCerboLombardi2025](#ref-AlbaneseDiCerboLombardi2025)]. Albanese, Di Cerbo and Lombardi also prove Singer vanishing for closed aspherical complex surfaces with residually finite fundamental group, and for those outside class $\mathrm{VII}_0^+$ without that hypothesis (Albanese, Di Cerbo, and Lombardi 2025, Theorems 1.5 and 4.2) <a id="pm-cite-14"></a>[[AlbaneseDiCerboLombardi2025](#ref-AlbaneseDiCerboLombardi2025)]. Their residual-finite argument uses the Albanese map, classification of complex surfaces and $L^2$ approximation. Class $\mathrm{VII}_0^+$ consists of minimal non-Kähler surfaces with Kodaira dimension $-\infty$ and positive second Betti number. The separate companion global spherical shell theorem (OpenAI 2026b, Theorem 1.1 and Corollary 1.2) <a id="pm-cite-15"></a>[[OpenAIGlobalSphericalShells2026](#ref-OpenAIGlobalSphericalShells2026)] gives these surfaces fundamental group $\mathbb Z$, excluding asphericity because an aspherical complex surface with that group would be homotopy equivalent to $S^1$ and have zero second Betti number. Combined with the cited Theorem 4.2, this gives an independent argument for the complex-surface case. It is not an input to the proof below.

Avramidi, Okun and Schreve prove the conjecture for the even-dimensional Gromov–Thurston cyclic branched covers arising from a closed oriented hyperbolic manifold and two separating transverse totally geodesic hypersurfaces (Avramidi, Okun, and Schreve 2025, sec. 3, p. 2628) <a id="pm-cite-16"></a>[[AvramidiOkunSchreve2025](#ref-AvramidiOkunSchreve2025)]. The branch locus is their intersection; the cyclic covering is specified by algebraic intersection with a chosen half of one hypersurface. Their cutting argument covers every positive branching degree in this even-dimensional setting. The proof here uses the algebraic consequences of integral Poincaré duality to construct a boundary and control its topology.

Finite-energy functions have a classical role in constructing boundaries. For graphs, the Royden compactification uses all bounded functions of finite Dirichlet energy (Keller et al. 2017, sec. 4) <a id="pm-cite-17"></a>[[KellerLenzSchmidtWojciechowski2017](#ref-KellerLenzSchmidtWojciechowski2017)]. We select a countable invariant family and add coordinates recording components of coordinate neighborhoods. This produces a metrizable boundary; its convergence dynamics and graph-path properties are proved directly. Bowditch’s complete median pretree and dendritic quotient organize the cut points (Bowditch 1999b, 1999a) <a id="pm-cite-18"></a>[[Bowditch1999Treelike](#ref-Bowditch1999Treelike), [Bowditch1999Connected](#ref-Bowditch1999Connected)]. The invariant finite-interval reduction in the all-parabolic case and the continuous retraction onto a block require the additional arguments given below. Our separation counts adapt Bowditch’s annulus-system method (Bowditch 1998, secs. 6–7) <a id="pm-cite-19"></a>[[Bowditch1998Topological](#ref-Bowditch1998Topological)]; the component labels and the threshold uniform over all sufficiently deep paths are proved in the present setting. The path-pushing strategy follows the Bestvina–Mess viewpoint as expounded by Hruska and Ruane (Hruska and Ruane 2024, sec. 1 and 3) <a id="pm-cite-20"></a>[[HruskaRuane2024](#ref-HruskaRuane2024)]. Here annular depth, component labels, and three anchors adapt that strategy to graphs that may have infinite valence and infinite stabilizers; the word-hyperbolic local-connectivity theorem is not being applied.

<a id="pm-h-4"></a>
#### The proof in outline

Suppose the first $L^2$-Betti number is positive. A finite presentation of $\Gamma$ gives a locally finite Cayley graph, and the corresponding reduced $L^2$-cohomology supplies a bounded function of finite Dirichlet energy whose behavior at infinity is nonconstant. Here finite energy means that the sum of the squared differences across all graph edges is finite.

We use translates of this function to build a compactification $X=\Gamma\sqcup B$. Its boundary $B$ is a nondegenerate continuum, meaning a compact connected metric space with more than one point. Additional coordinates distinguish the graph components of suitable coordinate neighborhoods. This construction ensures a precise connectivity property: vertices tending to the same boundary point can be joined by paths whose vertices tend uniformly to that point. This is a property of paths in the graph; local connectivity of $B$ will require a separate argument.

The square-summable edge lengths in $X$ control translated level sets. They give a *convergence action* on $B$: every sequence of distinct group elements has a subsequence converging uniformly on compact sets away from one boundary point to a constant. They also give $\dim B\le1$ for covering dimension. The group-ring cohomology vanishing supplied by Poincaré duality also gives $\check H^1(B;\mathbb F_2)=0$. A *cut point* is a point whose removal disconnects the space. If $B$ has no cut points, set $L=B$. Otherwise, Bowditch’s theory of pretrees reduces the problem to a subcontinuum $L\subset B$ without cut points. It is a retract: there is a continuous map $B\to L$ fixing every point of $L$. We give the required extension of the finite-interval reduction to parabolic cut points explicitly. The resulting $L$ remains nondegenerate and satisfies $$\dim L\le1,\qquad \check H^1(L;\mathbb F_2)=0.$$

The remaining step is local connectivity of $L$. We measure how deeply graph vertices lie in a boundary neighborhood by chains of nested annuli. Cohomology makes the nearby component labels well defined, and convergence dynamics makes those labels stable as the depth increases. A key point is uniformity: after fixing the neighborhood and a finite list of edge and stabilizer moves, one depth threshold works for every deeper graph path, independently of its endpoints and length. We obtain this threshold from three fixed boundary points and a majority-label argument. Thus paths can be pushed to arbitrary depth while their endpoints approach two prescribed nearby boundary points. Limits of these paths give small connected subsets of $L$. This argument applies even when the auxiliary graph has infinite stabilizers and infinite valence; it uses compactness of the ambient space in place of local finiteness of that graph.

Finally, the dimension and cohomology properties imply that $L$ contains no embedded circle. A locally connected compact metric continuum without an embedded circle is called a *dendrite*. Every nondegenerate dendrite has a cut point, giving the contradiction. The finite-energy compactification and the annular argument are the two constructions that connect the cohomological input to this continuum-theoretic conclusion.

The topological input is used before any boundary is constructed: a finite four-dimensional homotopy model supplies finite cellular chains, local-coefficient duality supplies the group-ring cohomology vanishings, and von Neumann dimension identifies the positive first $L^2$ class with a finite-energy gradient. Later, Bowditch’s pretree theorems apply to a continuum before local connectivity is known. The latter is obtained only after the retraction and uniform path argument. This order is essential, since the block stabilizer may be infinitely generated and its graph may have infinite valence.

Section <a href="#sec:foundations" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:foundations">2</a> establishes the algebraic and topological inputs. Sections <a href="#sec:compactification" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:compactification">3</a>–<a href="#sec:dynamics" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:dynamics">4</a> construct the boundary and its dynamics; Section <a href="#sec:boundary-topology" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:boundary-topology">5</a> proves its dimension and cohomology properties. Sections <a href="#sec:pretree" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:pretree">6</a>–<a href="#sec:blocks" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:blocks">7</a> remove cut points. Sections <a href="#sec:annuli" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:annuli">8</a>–<a href="#sec:path-pushing" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:path-pushing">9</a> prove local connectivity, Section <a href="#sec:conclusion" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:conclusion">10</a> completes the proof, and Section <a href="#sec:consequences" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:consequences">11</a> derives the consequences stated above.

<a id="sec:foundations"></a>
### Topological and algebraic reductions

Fix a finite connected aspherical integral Poincaré complex $Q$ of formal dimension four, with the absolute duality convention of the introduction, and put $\Gamma=\pi_1(Q)$ and $k=\mathbb F_2$. This section reduces Theorem <a href="#thm:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:main">1</a> to a statement about functions on a Cayley graph. We first justify the topological and coefficient conventions, then extract the group-theoretic restrictions needed below.

<a id="stmt-found-model"></a>
<div id="found:model" class="lemma">

**Lemma 3**. *The complex $Q$ is homotopy equivalent to a finite four-dimensional CW complex. The group $\Gamma$ is infinite, finitely presented, and torsion-free.*

*Every closed connected aspherical topological four-manifold is homotopy equivalent to a finite connected aspherical integral Poincaré complex of formal dimension four.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-found-model"><summary>Proof of stmt-found-model</summary>

*Proof.* Wall’s dimension theorem (Wall 1967, Theorem 2.2) <a id="pm-cite-21"></a>[[Wall1967](#ref-Wall1967)] gives a four-dimensional CW complex homotopy equivalent to $Q$. It can be chosen finite because $Q$ is finite, so its reduced finiteness obstruction vanishes. The model is aspherical, and its universal cover is contractible. Its augmented cellular chain complex is consequently a free $\mathbb Z\Gamma$-resolution of $\mathbb Z$ of length four. In particular, $\Gamma$ is finitely presented and has finite integral cohomological dimension. To see directly why it has no torsion, a finite-order element would yield a subgroup $C$ of prime order. Restricting the resolution to $\mathbb Z C$ preserves freeness and finite length. This contradicts the usual two-periodic resolution of a cyclic group, which gives nonzero groups $H^{2j}(C;\mathbb Z)$ for every $j\geq1$. If $\Gamma$ were finite, torsion-freeness would make it trivial, so asphericity would make $Q$ contractible. But duality over $k$ gives $H_4(Q;k)\cong H^0(Q;k)\cong k$. Hence $\Gamma$ is infinite.

For the manifold assertion, let $M$ be a closed connected aspherical topological four-manifold. A compact topological manifold is metrizable. Its coordinate neighborhoods are absolute neighborhood retracts, so Hanner’s locality theorem makes $M$ an absolute neighborhood retract; see (Hanner 1951, Theorems 3.2–3.3) <a id="pm-cite-22"></a>[[Hanner1951](#ref-Hanner1951)]. To check its covering dimension, shrink a finite coordinate cover to a finite closed cover. Each member has small inductive dimension at most four, by the subspace theorem and the dimension of Euclidean space. The closed-sum and coincidence theorems give $\dim M\le4$; conversely, a coordinate chart contains a copy of an open four-ball, so subspace monotonicity gives $\dim M\ge4$. These dimension facts are (Engelking 1978, Theorems 1.1.2, 1.5.3, 1.7.7 and 1.8.2) <a id="pm-cite-23"></a>[[Engelking1978](#ref-Engelking1978)]. West’s homotopy dimension estimate (West 1977, Corollary 5.4) <a id="pm-cite-24"></a>[[West1977](#ref-West1977)] therefore supplies a finite four-dimensional simplicial complex homotopy equivalent to $M$. This complex is a homotopy model, not a triangulation of the manifold. It is aspherical. Transport the orientation character and fundamental class of $M$ to this model. Topological Poincaré duality with the orientation local system gives cap-product isomorphisms for every local coefficient system on $M$ (Sun 2017, Theorem 1, with $R=\mathbb Z$) <a id="pm-cite-25"></a>[[Sun2017](#ref-Sun2017)]. Since $M$ is compact, compactly supported cohomology there is ordinary cohomology. Transporting these isomorphisms along the homotopy equivalence gives the required absolute integral duality on the model; see also (Spanier 1993, Theorem 10.2 and Remark 10.3) <a id="pm-cite-26"></a>[[Spanier1993](#ref-Spanier1993)], and (Friedl et al. 2025, Theorem A.16) <a id="pm-cite-27"></a>[[FNOP2025](#ref-FNOP2025)] for the oriented chain version. ◻

</details>

<a id="pm-h-6"></a>
#### The singular-chain definition and duality

We write $b_p^{(2)}(\widetilde Q)$ for the invariant defined using $\mathcal N(\Gamma)\otimes_{\mathbb Z\Gamma}C_*^s(\widetilde Q)$. The following comparison explains the passage from that definition to finite cellular matrices and, later, to a presentation graph.

<a id="stmt-found-comparison"></a>
<div id="found:comparison" class="lemma">

**Lemma 4**. *Let $\Lambda$ be a group and let $P_*$ be a free $\mathbb Z\Lambda$-resolution of $\mathbb Z$ whose terms in degrees $0,1,2$ are finitely generated. Suppose that a contractible space $Z$ has a free $\Lambda$-action for which its singular chains are free $\mathbb Z\Lambda$-modules. Then $$b_1^{(2)}(Z;\mathcal N(\Lambda))
 =\dim_{\mathcal N(\Lambda)}
   \bigl(\ker\partial_1\cap\ker\partial_2^*\bigr),$$ where the operators on the right act on the finite Hilbert modules $\ell^2(\Lambda)\otimes_{\mathbb Z\Lambda}P_i$. The same space represents first reduced Hilbert cohomology for $d_0=\partial_1^*$ and $d_1=\partial_2^*$. The analogous comparison holds in every degree of a finite free cellular complex.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-found-comparison"><summary>Proof of stmt-found-comparison</summary>

*Proof.* The augmented singular chains of $Z$ are a free resolution of $\mathbb Z$. The comparison theorem for free resolutions gives chain homotopy equivalences with $P_*$. Tensoring preserves these homotopies, so it preserves the homology in the definition of $b_1^{(2)}$; no flatness of $\mathcal N(\Lambda)$ over the group ring is required.

The remaining assertion is the finite-matrix comparison for von Neumann dimension. For a matrix over $\mathcal N(\Lambda)$, the algebraic kernel has dimension equal to the trace of the projection onto its Hilbert kernel. The algebraic image has dimension equal to the trace of the projection onto the closure of its Hilbert image. Additivity of dimension thus identifies the dimension of first algebraic homology with that of $$\ker\partial_1\ominus\overline{\operatorname{im}\partial_2}
 =\ker\partial_1\cap\ker\partial_2^*.$$ On the other hand, the harmonic representative space for reduced cohomology is $$\ker d_1\ominus\overline{\operatorname{im}d_0}
 =\ker\partial_2^*\cap\ker\partial_1.$$ These are the same closed Hilbert submodule. The same argument works in each degree with finite free neighboring terms. This is the comparison underlying (Lück 2002, Remark 1.31 and Lemmas 6.51–6.53) <a id="pm-cite-28"></a>[[Luck2002](#ref-Luck2002)]. ◻

</details>

<a id="stmt-found-reduction"></a>
<div id="found:reduction" class="lemma">

**Lemma 5**. *For the Poincaré complex $Q$ fixed above, $$b_0^{(2)}(\widetilde Q)=b_4^{(2)}(\widetilde Q)=0,
 \qquad
 b_3^{(2)}(\widetilde Q)=b_1^{(2)}(\widetilde Q),$$ and $b_p^{(2)}(\widetilde Q)=0$ for $p>4$. Consequently it suffices to prove $b_1^{(2)}(\widetilde Q)=0$.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-found-reduction"><summary>Proof of stmt-found-reduction</summary>

*Proof.* Use the finite four-dimensional model in Lemma <a href="#found:model" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="found:model">3</a>. A homotopy equivalence with $Q$ lifts to an equivariant homotopy equivalence of universal covers, and cellular and singular chains compute the same invariant; see (Lück 2002, Definition 6.50 and Lemmas 6.51–6.53) <a id="pm-cite-29"></a>[[Luck2002](#ref-Luck2002)]. There are no cellular chains above degree four, proving the assertion for $p>4$. A harmonic square-summable $0$-cochain on the connected universal cover is constant. Since $\Gamma$ is infinite, that constant must be zero. The degree-zero version of Lemma <a href="#found:comparison" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="found:comparison">4</a> gives $b_0^{(2)}=0$.

Here is the duality argument, including nontrivial orientation characters. Let $\Gamma^+$ be the kernel of the orientation character $\Gamma\to\{1,-1\}$, and let $Q^+$ be the corresponding connected cover. Its degree $d=[\Gamma:\Gamma^+]$ is either one or two. Finite-sheeted covers of Poincaré complexes are again Poincaré complexes of the same formal dimension (Stark 1996, 567, paragraph preceding Corollary 4.4) <a id="pm-cite-30"></a>[[Stark1996](#ref-Stark1996)]. The induced orientation character of $Q^+$ is the restriction to $\Gamma^+$ and is trivial. Thus $Q^+$ is a finite connected aspherical oriented integral Poincaré complex of formal dimension four, and Lemma <a href="#found:model" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="found:model">3</a> supplies a finite four-dimensional homotopy model $F$.

Transport the fundamental class of $Q^+$ to $F$. Poincaré duality with local coefficients applies to $Q^+$ and hence to $F$; see (Wall 1967, Lemma 1.1) <a id="pm-cite-31"></a>[[Wall1967](#ref-Wall1967)]. In particular, applying it to the regular group-ring local system shows that the cap-product map from the degree-reversed dual of $C_*(\widetilde F;\mathbb Z)$ to $C_*(\widetilde F;\mathbb Z)$ is a quasi-isomorphism. Here right modules are converted to left modules by the involution $g\mapsto g^{-1}$. Both complexes are bounded and finitely generated free over $\mathbb Z\Gamma^+$. The mapping cone is therefore a bounded acyclic complex of free modules, which is contractible: starting in its lowest degree, split each surjection onto the kernel already obtained. Thus cap product is a chain homotopy equivalence.

Extend scalars to $\mathcal N(\Gamma^+)$. The homotopies are preserved, and finite-matrix dimension comparison identifies dual Hilbert complexes with equal von Neumann dimensions. It follows that $$b_p^{(2)}(\widetilde Q;\mathcal N(\Gamma^+))
 =b_{4-p}^{(2)}(\widetilde Q;\mathcal N(\Gamma^+)),
 \qquad 0\leq p\leq4.$$ This is the finite-chain argument underlying the $L^2$-duality proof in (Lück 2002, Theorem 1.35(3) and its proof) <a id="pm-cite-32"></a>[[Luck2002](#ref-Luck2002)]; here Poincaré-complex duality supplies the required chain equivalence. Finally, finite-index scaling (Lück 2002, Theorem 1.35(9)) <a id="pm-cite-33"></a>[[Luck2002](#ref-Luck2002)] gives $$b_p^{(2)}(\widetilde Q;\mathcal N(\Gamma^+))
 =d\,b_p^{(2)}(\widetilde Q;\mathcal N(\Gamma)).$$ Dividing by $d$ proves the same duality for $Q$ and completes the reduction. ◻

</details>

<a id="pm-h-7"></a>
#### Cohomology, ends, and splittings

For the boundary argument we use mod-two coefficients. If $J\leq\Gamma$, write $k[\Gamma/J]$ for the left permutation module on left cosets. Its elements are finite sums of cosets.

<a id="stmt-found-duality"></a>
<div id="found:duality" class="lemma">
